</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.pathologic_mechanism_of_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Mitochondrial toxicity</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.pathologic_mechanism_of_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<i>ECHS1, ETHE1, HIBCH, L2HGDH, NAXE, SQOR</i>
</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.pathologic_mechanism_of_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Other</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.pathologic_mechanism_of_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><i>KGD4</i> (<i>MRPS36</i>)</td></tr></tbody></table></div><div><div><dl class="temp-labeled-list small"><dt></dt><dd><div><p class="no_margin">mtDNA = mitochondrial DNA</p></div></dd></dl></div></div></div></div></div><div id="leigh-nucl-ov.Differential_Diagnosis_of"><h2 id="_leigh-nucl-ov_Differential_Diagnosis_of_">3.  Differential Diagnosis of Nuclear Gene-Encoded Leigh Syndrome Spectrum</h2><p>The differential diagnosis of genetic causes of nuclear <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/gene/">gene</a>-encoded Leigh syndrome spectrum (LSS) include mitochondrial DNA-associated Leigh syndrome (see <a href="/sites/books/n/gene/narp/">Mitochondrial DNA-Associated Leigh Syndrome Spectrum</a> and <a href="/sites/books/n/gene/mt-overview/">Primary Mitochondrial Disorders Overview</a>).</p><p>Other non-mitochondrial monogenic disorders that cause or resemble LSS are listed in <a href="/sites/books/NBK320989/table/leigh-nucl-ov.T.nonmitochondrial_monogen/?report=objectonly" target="object" rid-ob="figobleighnuclovTnonmitochondrialmonogen">Table 6</a>.</p><div id="leigh-nucl-ov.T.nonmitochondrial_monogen" class="table"><h3><span class="label">Table 6. </span></h3><div class="caption"><p>Non-Mitochondrial Monogenic Disorders That Cause or Resemble Leigh Syndrome Spectrum</p></div><p class="large-table-link" style="display:none"><span class="right"><a href="/sites/books/NBK320989/table/leigh-nucl-ov.T.nonmitochondrial_monogen/?report=objectonly" target="object">View in own window</a></span></p><div class="large_tbl" id="__leigh-nucl-ov.T.nonmitochondrial_monogen_lrgtbl__"><table class="no_bottom_margin"><thead><tr><th id="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nonmitochondrial_monogen_1_1_1_1" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Key Feature</th><th id="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nonmitochondrial_monogen_1_1_1_2" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Gene(s)</th><th id="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nonmitochondrial_monogen_1_1_1_3" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Disorder</th><th id="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nonmitochondrial_monogen_1_1_1_4" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">MOI</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nonmitochondrial_monogen_1_1_1_1" rowspan="2" scope="rowgroup" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Infantile bilateral striatal necrosis</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nonmitochondrial_monogen_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<i>ADAR</i>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nonmitochondrial_monogen_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><i>ADAR</i>-related <a href="/sites/books/n/gene/ags/">Aicardi-Gouti&#x000e8;res syndrome</a></td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nonmitochondrial_monogen_1_1_1_4" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">AR<br />AD&#x000a0;<sup>1</sup></td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nonmitochondrial_monogen_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<i>NUP62</i>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nonmitochondrial_monogen_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><i>NUP62</i>-related infantile bilateral striatal necrosis (OMIM <a href="https://omim.org/entry/605815" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">605815</a>)</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nonmitochondrial_monogen_1_1_1_4" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">AR</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nonmitochondrial_monogen_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Infection-induced acute encephalopathy</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nonmitochondrial_monogen_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<i>RANBP2</i>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nonmitochondrial_monogen_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><i>RANBP2</i>-related susceptibility to infection-induced acute encephalopathy 3 (OMIM <a href="https://omim.org/entry/608033" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">608033</a>)</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nonmitochondrial_monogen_1_1_1_4" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">AD</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nonmitochondrial_monogen_1_1_1_1" rowspan="13" scope="rowgroup" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Neurodegenerative &#x00026;/or neurodevelopmental disorders with similar changes on neuroimaging</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nonmitochondrial_monogen_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<i>ALDH5A1</i>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nonmitochondrial_monogen_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<a href="/sites/books/n/gene/ssadh/">Succinic semialdehyde dehydrogenase deficiency</a>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nonmitochondrial_monogen_1_1_1_4" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">AR</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nonmitochondrial_monogen_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<i>ATP7B</i>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nonmitochondrial_monogen_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<a href="/sites/books/n/gene/wilson/">Wilson disease</a>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nonmitochondrial_monogen_1_1_1_4" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">AR</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nonmitochondrial_monogen_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<i>FTL</i>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nonmitochondrial_monogen_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<a href="/sites/books/n/gene/neuroferritin/">Neuroferritinopathy</a>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nonmitochondrial_monogen_1_1_1_4" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">AD</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nonmitochondrial_monogen_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<i>GAMT</i>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nonmitochondrial_monogen_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">GAMT deficiency (See <a href="/sites/books/n/gene/creatine/">Creatine Deficiency Disorders</a>.)</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nonmitochondrial_monogen_1_1_1_4" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">AR</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nonmitochondrial_monogen_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<i>GCDH</i>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nonmitochondrial_monogen_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<a href="/sites/books/n/gene/glutaric-a1/">Glutaric acidemia type 1</a>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nonmitochondrial_monogen_1_1_1_4" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">AR</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nonmitochondrial_monogen_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<i>MCEE</i>
<br />
<i>MMAA</i>
<br />
<i>MMAB</i>
<br />
<i>MMADHC</i>
<br />
<i>MMUT</i>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nonmitochondrial_monogen_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<a href="/sites/books/n/gene/mma/">Isolated methylmalonic acidemia</a>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nonmitochondrial_monogen_1_1_1_4" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">AR</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nonmitochondrial_monogen_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<i>MOCS1</i>
<br />
<i>MOCS2</i>
<br />
<i>MOCS3</i>
<br />
<i>GPHN</i>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nonmitochondrial_monogen_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<a href="/sites/books/n/gene/mc-def/">Molybdenum cofactor deficiency</a>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nonmitochondrial_monogen_1_1_1_4" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">AR</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nonmitochondrial_monogen_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<i>MORC2</i>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nonmitochondrial_monogen_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Developmental delay, impaired growth, <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/dysmorphic/">dysmorphic</a> facies, and axonal neuropathy (OMIM <a href="https://omim.org/entry/619090" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">619090</a>)</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nonmitochondrial_monogen_1_1_1_4" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">AD</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nonmitochondrial_monogen_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<i>PANK2</i>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nonmitochondrial_monogen_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<a href="/sites/books/n/gene/pkan/">Pantothenate kinase-associated neurodegeneration</a>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nonmitochondrial_monogen_1_1_1_4" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">AR</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nonmitochondrial_monogen_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<i>PCCA</i>
<br />
<i>PCCB</i>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nonmitochondrial_monogen_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<a href="/sites/books/n/gene/propionic-a/">Propionic acidemia</a>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nonmitochondrial_monogen_1_1_1_4" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">AR</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nonmitochondrial_monogen_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<i>RNF213</i>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nonmitochondrial_monogen_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Moyamoya disease 2, susceptibility to (OMIM <a href="https://omim.org/entry/607151" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">607151</a>)</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nonmitochondrial_monogen_1_1_1_4" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">AD<br />AR</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nonmitochondrial_monogen_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<i>SLC39A8</i>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nonmitochondrial_monogen_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<a href="/sites/books/n/gene/slc39a8-cdg/">SLC39A8-CDG</a>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nonmitochondrial_monogen_1_1_1_4" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">AR</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nonmitochondrial_monogen_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<i>SUOX</i>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nonmitochondrial_monogen_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<a href="/sites/books/n/gene/iso-def/">Isolated sulfite oxidase deficiency</a>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nonmitochondrial_monogen_1_1_1_4" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">AR</td></tr></tbody></table></div><div><div><dl class="temp-labeled-list small"><dt></dt><dd><div><p class="no_margin">AD = <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/autosomal-dominant/">autosomal dominant</a>; AR = <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/autosomal-recessive/">autosomal recessive</a>; CDG = <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/congenital/">congenital</a> disorder of glycosylation; MOI = <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/mode-of-inheritance/">mode of inheritance</a></p></div></dd><dt>1. </dt><dd><div id="leigh-nucl-ov.TF.6.1"><p class="no_margin"><i>ADAR</i>-related <a href="/sites/books/n/gene/ags/">Aicardi-Gouti&#x000e8;res syndrome</a> can be inherited in an <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/autosomal-recessive/">autosomal recessive</a> or <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/autosomal-dominant/">autosomal dominant</a> manner depending on the specific <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a>.</p></div></dd></dl></div></div></div><p>Acquired non-genetic conditions in the differential diagnosis of LSS include viral encephalopathy, hypoxic-ischemic encephalopathy, Wernicke encephalopathy secondary to thiamine deficiency, and acute necrotizing encephalopathy (which may be triggered by viral infection).</p></div><div id="leigh-nucl-ov.Evaluation_Strategies_to_I"><h2 id="_leigh-nucl-ov_Evaluation_Strategies_to_I_">4.  Evaluation Strategies to Identify the Genetic Cause of Nuclear Gene-Encoded Leigh Syndrome Spectrum in a Proband</h2><p>Establishing a specific genetic cause of nuclear <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/gene/">gene</a>-encoded LSS:</p><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Can aid in discussions of prognosis (which are beyond the scope of this <i>GeneReview</i>) and <a href="#leigh-nucl-ov.Genetic_Counseling">genetic counseling</a>;</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Usually involves a medical history, physical examination, imaging studies such as brain MRI, biochemical testing, family history, and <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/genomic/">genomic</a>/genetic testing.</div></li></ul><p>While some clinical features are associated with some specific nuclear-encoded LSS genes (see <a href="/sites/books/NBK320989/table/leigh-nucl-ov.T.autosomal_recessive_leig/?report=objectonly" target="object" rid-ob="figobleighnuclovTautosomalrecessiveleig">Tables 2a</a>, <a href="/sites/books/NBK320989/table/leigh-nucl-ov.T.autosomal_recessive_leig_1/?report=objectonly" target="object" rid-ob="figobleighnuclovTautosomalrecessiveleig1">2b</a>, <a href="/sites/books/NBK320989/table/leigh-nucl-ov.T.autosomal_dominant_leigh/?report=objectonly" target="object" rid-ob="figobleighnuclovTautosomaldominantleigh">3</a>, <a href="/sites/books/NBK320989/table/leigh-nucl-ov.T.xlinked_leigh_syndrome_s/?report=objectonly" target="object" rid-ob="figobleighnuclovTxlinkedleighsyndromes">4a</a>, and <a href="/sites/books/NBK320989/table/leigh-nucl-ov.T.xlinked_leigh_syndrome_s_1/?report=objectonly" target="object" rid-ob="figobleighnuclovTxlinkedleighsyndromes1">4b</a>), the significant overlap in the clinical and/or biochemical features alone among the causative genes makes it difficult to narrow down the likely molecular diagnosis based on these findings. Additionally, there is significant clinical overlap between nuclear-encoded LSS and mtDNA-encoded LSS. Hence, it would be unusual for specific clinical and/or imaging findings to guide testing of a subset of genes.</p><p><b>Medical history.</b> A detailed history should be taken, particularly in respect to neurologic features. Exacerbation of manifestations or periods of regression during times of metabolic stress (e.g., viral illness, surgery, and/or fasting) should be explored, as this is highly suggestive of nuclear-encoded LSS. See <a href="/sites/books/NBK320989/table/leigh-nucl-ov.T.autosomal_recessive_leig/?report=objectonly" target="object" rid-ob="figobleighnuclovTautosomalrecessiveleig">Tables 2a</a>, <a href="/sites/books/NBK320989/table/leigh-nucl-ov.T.autosomal_recessive_leig_1/?report=objectonly" target="object" rid-ob="figobleighnuclovTautosomalrecessiveleig1">2b</a>, <a href="/sites/books/NBK320989/table/leigh-nucl-ov.T.autosomal_dominant_leigh/?report=objectonly" target="object" rid-ob="figobleighnuclovTautosomaldominantleigh">3</a>, <a href="/sites/books/NBK320989/table/leigh-nucl-ov.T.xlinked_leigh_syndrome_s/?report=objectonly" target="object" rid-ob="figobleighnuclovTxlinkedleighsyndromes">4a</a>, and <a href="/sites/books/NBK320989/table/leigh-nucl-ov.T.xlinked_leigh_syndrome_s_1/?report=objectonly" target="object" rid-ob="figobleighnuclovTxlinkedleighsyndromes1">4b</a> for further <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/gene/">gene</a>-specific information.</p><p><b>Physical examination.</b> There are no pathognomonic clinical signs of nuclear-encoded LSS. A standard physical examination as well as detailed neurologic and ophthalmologic examinations should be completed in all individuals.</p><p>Some examples of specific clinical features that may increase suspicion for a particular <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/gene/">gene</a> include hypertrichosis (suggestive of <i>SURF1</i>-related LSS). See <a href="/sites/books/NBK320989/table/leigh-nucl-ov.T.autosomal_recessive_leig/?report=objectonly" target="object" rid-ob="figobleighnuclovTautosomalrecessiveleig">Tables 2a</a>, <a href="/sites/books/NBK320989/table/leigh-nucl-ov.T.autosomal_recessive_leig_1/?report=objectonly" target="object" rid-ob="figobleighnuclovTautosomalrecessiveleig1">2b</a>, <a href="/sites/books/NBK320989/table/leigh-nucl-ov.T.autosomal_dominant_leigh/?report=objectonly" target="object" rid-ob="figobleighnuclovTautosomaldominantleigh">3</a>, <a href="/sites/books/NBK320989/table/leigh-nucl-ov.T.xlinked_leigh_syndrome_s/?report=objectonly" target="object" rid-ob="figobleighnuclovTxlinkedleighsyndromes">4a</a>, and <a href="/sites/books/NBK320989/table/leigh-nucl-ov.T.xlinked_leigh_syndrome_s_1/?report=objectonly" target="object" rid-ob="figobleighnuclovTxlinkedleighsyndromes1">4b</a> for additional gene-specific information.</p><p><b>Imaging.</b> Characteristic patterns of brain lesions on imaging described for some specific nuclear-encoded LSS genes include the following:</p><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>NDUFAF2 deficiency is associated with brain stem lesions seen within the mamillothalamic tracts, substantia nigra, medial lemniscus, medial longitudinal fasciculus, and spinothalamic tracts on T<sub>2</sub>-weighted MRI [<a class="bk_pop" href="#leigh-nucl-ov.REF.fassone.2012.578">Fassone &#x00026; Rahman 2012</a>].</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div><a href="/sites/books/n/gene/megdel/">SERAC1 deficiency</a> is associated with a distinctive brain MRI pattern affecting the basal ganglia, especially the putamen. Initially there are T<sub>2</sub>-weighted signal changes of the pallidum, and later swelling of the putamen and caudate nucleus with an "eye" representing early sparing of the dorsal putamen, followed by progressive involvement of the putamina.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Brain malformations are typically seen in males with a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/hemizygous/">hemizygous</a> <i>PDHA1</i> <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> and some females with a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/heterozygous/">heterozygous</a> <i>PDHA1</i> pathogenic variant [<a class="bk_pop" href="#leigh-nucl-ov.REF.patel.2012.385">Patel et al 2012</a>].</div></li></ul><p><b>Family history.</b> A three-generation family history should be taken with attention to relatives with manifestations of nuclear <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/gene/">gene</a>-encoded LSS, or other clinical features compatible with a mitochondrial disorder. Relevant findings can be identified from direct examination of the individual or review of medical records, including results of <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/molecular-genetic-testing/">molecular genetic testing</a>, neuroimaging studies, and/or autopsy examinations.</p><div id="leigh-nucl-ov.Biochemical_and_GenomicGen"><h3>Biochemical and Genomic/Genetic Testing</h3><p><b>Testing for treatable disorders.</b> Disorders with targeted therapy (see <a href="/sites/books/NBK320989/table/leigh-nucl-ov.T.autosomal_recessive_leig/?report=objectonly" target="object" rid-ob="figobleighnuclovTautosomalrecessiveleig">Tables 2a</a> and <a href="/sites/books/NBK320989/table/leigh-nucl-ov.T.xlinked_leigh_syndrome_s/?report=objectonly" target="object" rid-ob="figobleighnuclovTxlinkedleighsyndromes">4a</a>) should be rapidly tested biochemically or genetically as indicated; if this is not possible, trials of the relevant vitamins/cofactors should be instituted as soon as the diagnosis is considered. Ideally, therapy should continue until these disorders have been excluded by biochemical and/or genetic testing, and continued for life if the diagnosis is confirmed.</p><div id="leigh-nucl-ov.Biochemical_Testing"><h4>Biochemical Testing</h4><p>Elevated blood and/or cerebrospinal fluid (CSF) lactate concentrations may strongly support the diagnosis of nuclear-encoded LSS.</p><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Note: A normal blood and/or CSF lactate concentration does not exclude nuclear-encoded LSS.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>A normal to low lactate-to-pyruvate ratio may indicate a disorder associated with pyruvate dehydrogenase complex deficiency.</div></li></ul><p>Plasma amino acids may show:</p><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Increased alanine concentration, reflecting persistent hyperlactatemia;</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Elevated glycine concentrations that may indicate a defect in lipoic acid biosynthesis (except LIPT1 deficiency).</div></li></ul><p>Acylcarnitine profile may be abnormal, with characteristic patterns associated with the following disorders:</p><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Elevated hydroxy-C4-carnitine may be associated with HIBCH deficiency;</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Elevated C3-carnitine may be associated with SUCLG1 and SUCLA2 deficiencies.</div></li></ul><p>Abnormalities in urine organic acids may be nonspecific and may show lactic aciduria, increased Krebs cycle intermediates, and increased dicarboxylic acids. However, some <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/gene/">gene</a> defects are associated with a specific urine metabolite profile, including the following:</p><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>SERAC1, CLPB, and HTRA2 deficiencies are associated with 3-methylglutaconic aciduria.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>SUCLG1 and SUCLA2 deficiencies are associated with methylmalonic aciduria.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>ETHE1 deficiency is associated with ethylmalonic aciduria.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>ECHS1 and HIBCH deficiencies are associated with marked elevation of urine 2-methyl-2,3-dihydroxybutyrate and S-(2-carboxypropyl) cysteine.</div></li></ul><p>See <a href="/sites/books/NBK320989/table/leigh-nucl-ov.T.autosomal_recessive_leig/?report=objectonly" target="object" rid-ob="figobleighnuclovTautosomalrecessiveleig">Tables 2a</a>, <a href="/sites/books/NBK320989/table/leigh-nucl-ov.T.autosomal_recessive_leig_1/?report=objectonly" target="object" rid-ob="figobleighnuclovTautosomalrecessiveleig1">2b</a>, <a href="/sites/books/NBK320989/table/leigh-nucl-ov.T.autosomal_dominant_leigh/?report=objectonly" target="object" rid-ob="figobleighnuclovTautosomaldominantleigh">3</a>, <a href="/sites/books/NBK320989/table/leigh-nucl-ov.T.xlinked_leigh_syndrome_s/?report=objectonly" target="object" rid-ob="figobleighnuclovTxlinkedleighsyndromes">4a</a>, and <a href="/sites/books/NBK320989/table/leigh-nucl-ov.T.xlinked_leigh_syndrome_s_1/?report=objectonly" target="object" rid-ob="figobleighnuclovTxlinkedleighsyndromes1">4b</a> for further <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/gene/">gene</a>-specific information.</p><p><b>Measurement of enzyme activity.</b> Traditionally tissue biopsy was the first-line diagnostic test in the investigation of LSS and other mitochondrial disorders. However, it is an invasive procedure that often requires general anesthesia, which increases the risk of a metabolic decompensation. Although the widespread use of <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/genomic/">genomic</a> testing has obviated the need for muscle biopsy in many instances, in a small minority of individuals, enzymatic testing of a muscle or skin biopsy may be necessary to confirm the pathogenicity of variants of <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/uncertain-significance/">uncertain significance</a> identified by genomic testing.</p><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Activity of enzymes, such as pyruvate dehydrogenase (PDH), are typically measured in cultured skin fibroblasts (fb), and respiratory chain enzymes are typically measured in a skeletal muscle biopsy (m); see <a href="/sites/books/NBK320989/table/leigh-nucl-ov.T.autosomal_recessive_leig/?report=objectonly" target="object" rid-ob="figobleighnuclovTautosomalrecessiveleig">Tables 2a</a>, <a href="/sites/books/NBK320989/table/leigh-nucl-ov.T.autosomal_recessive_leig_1/?report=objectonly" target="object" rid-ob="figobleighnuclovTautosomalrecessiveleig1">2b</a>, <a href="/sites/books/NBK320989/table/leigh-nucl-ov.T.autosomal_dominant_leigh/?report=objectonly" target="object" rid-ob="figobleighnuclovTautosomaldominantleigh">3</a>, <a href="/sites/books/NBK320989/table/leigh-nucl-ov.T.xlinked_leigh_syndrome_s/?report=objectonly" target="object" rid-ob="figobleighnuclovTxlinkedleighsyndromes">4a</a>, and <a href="/sites/books/NBK320989/table/leigh-nucl-ov.T.xlinked_leigh_syndrome_s_1/?report=objectonly" target="object" rid-ob="figobleighnuclovTxlinkedleighsyndromes1">4b</a> for further <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/gene/">gene</a>-specific information.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Respiratory chain enzymology can identify deficient enzyme activity (&#x0003c;30% of control mean values) of one or more of the respiratory chain enzyme complexes. Complex I and Complex IV deficiencies are the most common enzyme abnormalities observed in individuals with nuclear-encoded LSS.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Although identifying deficiency of an enzyme can help prioritize <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/molecular-genetic-testing/">molecular genetic testing</a>, this approach still leaves a large number of genes to be tested (e.g., respiratory chain complex I-deficient LSS has been shown to be caused by pathogenic variants in at least 28 nuclear genes and six mtDNA genes).</div></li></ul></div><div id="leigh-nucl-ov.Molecular_Genetic_Testing"><h4>Molecular Genetic Testing</h4><p>Molecular genetic testing approaches can include a combination of <b><a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/gene/">gene</a>-targeted testing</b> (<a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/multigene-panel/">multigene panel</a>) and <b>comprehensive</b>
<b><a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/genomic/">genomic</a> testing</b> (<a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/exome-sequencing/">exome sequencing</a>, <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/genome-sequencing/">genome sequencing</a>). Choice of approach may depend on clinical suspicion and availability of testing. Gene-targeted testing requires that the clinician determine which gene(s) are likely involved (see <a href="#leigh-nucl-ov.Option_1">Option 1</a>); however, this approach in the diagnosis of nuclear gene-encoded LSS may be difficult given the significant clinical overlap between different genes. Comprehensive genomic testing does not require a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/phenotype/">phenotype</a>-driven hypothesis (see <a href="#leigh-nucl-ov.Option_2">Option 2</a>).</p><div id="leigh-nucl-ov.Option_1"><h5>
<b>Option 1</b>
</h5><p><b>A</b>
<b>mitochondrial disorders</b>
<b><a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/multigene-panel/">multigene panel</a></b> that includes, at a minimum, genes associated with disorders that have targeted therapy (see <a href="/sites/books/NBK320989/table/leigh-nucl-ov.T.autosomal_recessive_leig/?report=objectonly" target="object" rid-ob="figobleighnuclovTautosomalrecessiveleig">Tables 2a</a> and <a href="/sites/books/NBK320989/table/leigh-nucl-ov.T.xlinked_leigh_syndrome_s/?report=objectonly" target="object" rid-ob="figobleighnuclovTxlinkedleighsyndromes">4a</a>) as well as some or all of the genes included in <a href="#leigh-nucl-ov.Causes_of_Nuclear_GeneEnco">Section 2</a> and other genes of interest (see <a href="#leigh-nucl-ov.Differential_Diagnosis_of">Section 3</a>) is most likely to identify the genetic cause of the condition while limiting identification of pathogenic variants and variants of <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/uncertain-significance/">uncertain significance</a> in genes that do not explain the underlying <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/phenotype/">phenotype</a>. Note: (1) The genes included in the panel and the diagnostic <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/sensitivity/">sensitivity</a> of the testing used for each <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/gene/">gene</a> vary by laboratory and are likely to change over time. (2) Some multigene panels may include genes not associated with the condition discussed in this <i>GeneReview</i>. (3) In some laboratories, panel options may include a custom laboratory-designed panel and/or custom phenotype-focused <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/exome/">exome</a> analysis that includes genes specified by the clinician. (4) Methods used in a panel may include <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/sequence-analysis/">sequence analysis</a>, <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/deletion-duplication-analysis/">deletion/duplication analysis</a>, and/or other non-sequencing-based tests.</p><p>For an introduction to multigene panels click <a href="/sites/books/n/gene/app5/#app5.Multigene_Panels">here</a>. More detailed information for clinicians ordering genetic tests can be found <a href="/sites/books/n/gene/app5/#app5.Multigene_Panels_FAQs">here</a>.</p></div><div id="leigh-nucl-ov.Option_2"><h5>
<b>Option 2</b>
</h5><p><b>Comprehensive <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/genomic/">genomic</a> testing</b> does not require the clinician to determine which <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/gene/">gene</a> is likely involved and may provide or suggest a diagnosis not previously considered (e.g., variant in a different gene[s] that results in a similar clinical <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/phenotype/">phenotype</a>). <b>Exome sequencing</b> is most commonly used; <b><a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/genome-sequencing/">genome sequencing</a></b> is also possible.</p><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Genome sequencing is preferred (if available), as it allows analysis of the mitochondrial and nuclear genome, and typically has the highest diagnostic rate. About 30% of children with suspected LSS have mtDNA-encoded LSS (see <a href="#leigh-nucl-ov.Differential_Diagnosis_of">Section 3</a>).</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>When testing children, family trio <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/genome-sequencing/">genome sequencing</a> should be considered to assist in the identification of either <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/biallelic/">biallelic</a> pathogenic variants that cause rare <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/autosomal-recessive/">autosomal recessive</a> disorders or <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/de-novo/"><i>de novo</i></a> pathogenic variants.</div></li></ul><p>For an introduction to comprehensive <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/genomic/">genomic</a> testing click <a href="/sites/books/n/gene/app5/#app5.Comprehensive_Genomic_Testing">here</a>. More detailed information for clinicians ordering genomic testing can be found <a href="/sites/books/n/gene/app5/#app5.Comprehensive_Genomic_Testing_1">here</a>.</p></div></div></div></div><div id="leigh-nucl-ov.Management_of_Nuclear_Gene"><h2 id="_leigh-nucl-ov_Management_of_Nuclear_Gene_">5.  Management of Nuclear Gene-Encoded Leigh Syndrome</h2><div id="leigh-nucl-ov.Treatment_of_Manifestation"><h3>Treatment of Manifestations</h3><div id="leigh-nucl-ov.Targeted_Therapies"><h4>Targeted Therapies</h4><p><i>In GeneReviews, a targeted therapy
is one that addresses the specific underlying mechanism of disease causation (regardless of whether the therapy is significantly efficacious for one or more manifestation of the genetic condition); would otherwise not be considered without knowledge of the underlying genetic cause of the condition; or could lead to a cure</i>. &#x02014;ED</p><p>Targeted therapies are available for some specific nuclear <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/gene/">gene</a>-encoded Leigh syndrome spectrum (LSS) disorders, listed in <a href="/sites/books/NBK320989/table/leigh-nucl-ov.T.leigh_syndrome_spectrum/?report=objectonly" target="object" rid-ob="figobleighnuclovTleighsyndromespectrum">Table 7</a>.</p><div id="leigh-nucl-ov.T.leigh_syndrome_spectrum" class="table"><h3><span class="label">Table 7. </span></h3><div class="caption"><p>Leigh Syndrome Spectrum Disorders with Targeted Therapies</p></div><p class="large-table-link" style="display:none"><span class="right"><a href="/sites/books/NBK320989/table/leigh-nucl-ov.T.leigh_syndrome_spectrum/?report=objectonly" target="object">View in own window</a></span></p><div class="large_tbl" id="__leigh-nucl-ov.T.leigh_syndrome_spectrum_lrgtbl__"><table><thead><tr><th id="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.leigh_syndrome_spectrum_1_1_1_1" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Disorder</th><th id="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.leigh_syndrome_spectrum_1_1_1_2" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Treatment</th><th id="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.leigh_syndrome_spectrum_1_1_1_3" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Comments</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.leigh_syndrome_spectrum_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><a href="/sites/books/n/gene/bgd-biotin/">Biotin-thiamine-responsive basal ganglia disease</a> (thiamine transporter-2 deficiency)</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.leigh_syndrome_spectrum_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Biotin &#x00026; thiamine supplementation</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.leigh_syndrome_spectrum_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Manifestations typically resolve within days.</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.leigh_syndrome_spectrum_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<a href="/sites/books/n/gene/biotin/">Biotinidase deficiency</a>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.leigh_syndrome_spectrum_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Biotin supplementation</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.leigh_syndrome_spectrum_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>All persons w/profound biotinidase deficiency (&#x0003c;10% mean normal serum enzyme activity) &#x00026; those w/partial biotinidase deficiency (10%-30% of mean normal serum enzyme activity) should be treated w/oral biotin in the free form as opposed to the protein-bound form.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Persons w/biotinidase deficiency who are diagnosed before they have developed manifestations (e.g., by newborn screening) &#x00026; who are treated w/biotin have normal development.</div></li></ul>
</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.leigh_syndrome_spectrum_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<a href="/sites/books/n/gene/coq10-def/">Primary coenzyme Q<sub>10</sub> deficiency</a>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.leigh_syndrome_spectrum_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Coenzyme Q<sub>10</sub> supplementation</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.leigh_syndrome_spectrum_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Treatment should be instituted as early as possible because it can limit disease progression &#x00026; reverse some manifestations; however, established severe neurologic &#x00026;/or kidney damage cannot be reversed.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Response is highly variable, &#x00026; depends on both the specific genetic defect &#x00026; disease severity, but also other unknown factors.</div></li></ul>
</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.leigh_syndrome_spectrum_1_1_1_1" rowspan="2" scope="rowgroup" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<a href="/sites/books/n/gene/pdhc-def-ov/">Primary pyruvate dehydrogenase complex deficiency</a>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.leigh_syndrome_spectrum_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Ketogenic diet</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.leigh_syndrome_spectrum_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Response to ketogenic diet has been shown to be most beneficial in persons w/milder disease.</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.leigh_syndrome_spectrum_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Thiamine</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.leigh_syndrome_spectrum_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Thiamine has been used w/limited success. Response is variable &#x00026; depends on the specific genetic defect.</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.leigh_syndrome_spectrum_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<a href="/sites/books/n/gene/amish-mcph/"><i>SLC25A19</i>-related thiamine metabolism dysfunction</a>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.leigh_syndrome_spectrum_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Thiamine</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.leigh_syndrome_spectrum_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Thiamine treatment is lifelong.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Thiamine dose must be &#x02191; during febrile illness, surgery, or acute decompensation (by 25%).</div></li></ul>
</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.leigh_syndrome_spectrum_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<a href="/sites/books/n/gene/tpk1-def/"><i>TPK1</i>-related thiamine metabolism dysfunction syndrome</a>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.leigh_syndrome_spectrum_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Thiamine</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.leigh_syndrome_spectrum_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Variable response to thiamine among affected persons, w/response reported in approximately 50% of individuals [<a class="bk_pop" href="#leigh-nucl-ov.REF.zhao.2023.1173787">Zhao et al 2023</a>].</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>More effective with early diagnosis</div></li></ul>
</td></tr></tbody></table></div></div></div><div id="leigh-nucl-ov.Supportive_Treatment"><h4>Supportive Treatment</h4><p>Supportive care to improve quality of life, maximize function, and reduce complications is recommended. This ideally involves multidisciplinary care by specialists in relevant fields (see <a href="/sites/books/NBK320989/table/leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig/?report=objectonly" target="object" rid-ob="figobleighnuclovTnucleargeneencodedleig">Table 8</a>).</p><div id="leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig" class="table"><h3><span class="label">Table 8. </span></h3><div class="caption"><p>Nuclear Gene-Encoded Leigh Syndrome Spectrum: Treatment of Manifestations</p></div><p class="large-table-link" style="display:none"><span class="right"><a href="/sites/books/NBK320989/table/leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig/?report=objectonly" target="object">View in own window</a></span></p><div class="large_tbl" id="__leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_lrgtbl__"><table class="no_bottom_margin"><thead><tr><th id="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_1" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Manifestation/Concern</th><th id="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_2" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Treatment</th><th id="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_3" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Considerations/Other</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Acute acidosis</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Sodium bicarbonate or sodium citrate for significant acidosis</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Consider THAM if hypernatremia occurs.</div></li></ul>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Avoidance of lactate-containing solutions (e.g., Ringer's lactate)</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Epilepsy</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Treatment w/ASM by experienced neurologist</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Sodium valproate should be avoided because of its inhibitory effects on the mitochondrial respiratory chain.&#x000a0;<sup>1</sup></div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Education of parents/caregivers&#x000a0;<sup>2</sup></div></li></ul>
</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_1" rowspan="2" scope="rowgroup" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<p>
<b>Dystonia</b>
</p>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Benzhexol, baclofen, tetrabenazine, &#x00026; gabapentin may be useful, alone or in various combinations.</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Initial dose should be low &#x00026; gradually &#x02191; until manifestations are controlled, or intolerable side effects occur.</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Botulinum toxin injection has been used in persons w/LSS &#x00026; severe intractable dystonia</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Should be used w/caution given potential side effects of exacerbating muscle weakness.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Should not be used in head &#x00026; neck because of risk of adverse events (e.g., impaired swallow).</div></li></ul>
</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Pulmonology</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Referral to pulmonologist as needed</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Ventilatory support for persons w/LSS &#x00026; respiratory compromise</div></li></ul>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"></td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Feeding/Nutrition</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Caloric &#x00026; nutritional supplementation as needed (incl micronutrients)</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Feeding therapy as needed; gastrostomy tube placement may improve nutritional intake for those w/persistent feeding issues, choking, or aspiration risk due to dysphagia</div></li></ul>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Low threshold for clinical feeding eval &#x00026;/or radiographic swallowing study when showing clinical signs or symptoms of dysphagia</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>While ketogenic diet may be indicated for persons w/PDH deficiency or drug-resistant epilepsy, there is no evidence for efficacy of ketogenic diet in other forms of LSS.</div></li></ul>
</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Development/Cognition</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">See <a href="/sites/books/NBK320989/#leigh-nucl-ov-dd_id_mgmt.pdf">Developmental Delay&#x000a0;/ Intellectual Disability Management Issues</a>.</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"></td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Myopathy &#x00026;/or ataxia</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">PT &#x00026; OT</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Equipment to prevent falling, maintain independent mobility as appropriate</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Eyes</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Standard treatments for eye movement disorders as advised by ophthalmologist</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"></td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Cardiomyopathy</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Anticongestive therapy or antiarrhythmic therapy may be required &#x00026; treatment should be managed by a cardiologist.</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"></td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Gastrointestinal</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Stool softeners, laxatives for constipation as needed</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"></td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Liver</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Treatments for liver failure as advised by hepatologist</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"></td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Renal</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Electrolyte monitoring &#x00026; replacement for renal tubular losses as advised by nephrologist</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"></td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Hearing</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Hearing aids or cochlear implants for SNHL</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Referral to speech-language therapist</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Referral to hearing support services</div></li></ul>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Counsel to avoid excessive noise exposure, which can exacerbate hearing loss.</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Endocrine</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Treatment for diabetes mellitus or adrenal insufficiency managed by endocrinologist</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"></td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Neurobehavioral/</b>
<br />
<b>Psychiatric</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Standard treatments for anxiety &#x00026;/or depression</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Psychological support for affected person &#x00026; family is essential.</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Family/Community</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Ensure appropriate social work involvement to connect families w/local resources, respite, &#x00026; support.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Coordinate care to manage multiple subspecialty appointments, equipment, medications, &#x00026; supplies.</div></li></ul>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Ongoing assessment of need for palliative care involvement &#x00026;/or home nursing</td></tr></tbody></table></div><div><div><dl class="temp-labeled-list small"><dt></dt><dd><div><p class="no_margin">ASM = anti-seizure medication; LSS = Leigh syndrome spectrum; OT = occupational therapy; PDH = pyruvate dehydrogenase; PT = physical therapy; SNHL = sensorineural hearing loss; THAM = tris-hydroxymethyl aminomethane</p></div></dd><dt>1. </dt><dd><div id="leigh-nucl-ov.TF.8.1"><p class="no_margin">
<a class="bk_pop" href="#leigh-nucl-ov.REF.de_vries.2020.800">De Vries et al [2020]</a>
</p></div></dd><dt>2. </dt><dd><div id="leigh-nucl-ov.TF.8.2"><p class="no_margin">Education of parents/caregivers regarding common seizure presentations is appropriate. For information on non-medical interventions and coping strategies for children diagnosed with epilepsy, see <a href="https://www.epilepsy.com/tools-resources/forms-resources#Epilepsy-Foundation-Toolbox" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">Epilepsy Foundation Toolbox</a>.</p></div></dd></dl></div></div></div></div></div><div id="leigh-nucl-ov.Surveillance"><h3>Surveillance</h3><p>Affected individuals should be followed at regular intervals (typically every 6-12 months) to monitor progression of known manifestations and the appearance of new manifestations. Neurologic, ophthalmologic, audiologic, and cardiologic evaluations are recommended (see <a href="/sites/books/NBK320989/table/leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1/?report=objectonly" target="object" rid-ob="figobleighnuclovTnucleargeneencodedleig1">Table 9</a>). Surveillance may be directed by knowledge of phenotypes known to be associated with specific <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/gene/">gene</a> defects [<a class="bk_pop" href="#leigh-nucl-ov.REF.rahman.2017.9">Rahman et al 2017</a>].</p><div id="leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1" class="table"><h3><span class="label">Table 9. </span></h3><div class="caption"><p>Nuclear Gene-Encoded Leigh Syndrome Spectrum: Recommended Surveillance</p></div><p class="large-table-link" style="display:none"><span class="right"><a href="/sites/books/NBK320989/table/leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1/?report=objectonly" target="object">View in own window</a></span></p><div class="large_tbl" id="__leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_lrgtbl__"><table class="no_bottom_margin"><thead><tr><th id="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_1_1" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">System/Concern</th><th id="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_1_2" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Evaluation</th><th id="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_1_3" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Frequency</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Neurologic</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Neurology assessment for ataxia/movement disorders, neuropathy, &#x00026; seizures</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_1_3" rowspan="4" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">At each visit</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Pulmonary</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Monitor for evidence of aspiration, nocturnal hypoventilation, or sleep-disordered breathing.</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Feeding/Nutrition</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Measurement of growth parameters</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Eval of nutritional status (incl micronutrients) &#x00026; safety of oral intake</div></li></ul>
</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Gastroenterology</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Assess for nausea, constipation, or dysmotility.</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Development/</b>
<br />
<b>Cognition</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Monitor developmental progress, educational needs, &#x00026; cognitive issues.</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_1_3" rowspan="2" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">At least annually</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Musculoskeletal</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Physical medicine, OT/PT assessment of mobility, self-help skills</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Ophthalmology</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Ophthalmology eval</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Every 6-12 months or as advised by ophthalmologist</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Cardiac</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Blood pressure</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Electrocardiogram</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Echocardiogram</div></li></ul>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Annually or as advised by cardiologist</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Liver</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Liver function tests incl transaminases, albumin, bilirubin, &#x00026; coagulation studies</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_1_3" rowspan="4" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Annually</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Renal</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Urinalysis, urine albumin-to-creatinine ratio, urine amino acids</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Serum electrolytes, BUN, creatinine</div></li></ul>
</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Hearing</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Audiology eval</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Hematology</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Complete blood count</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Endocrine</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Fasting glucose</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Consider hemoglobin A1c or OGTT if clinical features consistent w/diabetes mellitus.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Consider early morning cortisol &#x00026; ACTH stimulation test if concerns about adrenal insufficiency.</div></li></ul>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Annually</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Family/Community</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:top;">Assess family need for social work support (e.g., palliative/respite care, home nursing, other local resources), care coordination, or follow-up <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/genetic-counseling/">genetic counseling</a> if new questions arise (e.g., family planning).</td><td headers="hd_h_leigh-nucl-ov.T.nuclear_geneencoded_leig_1_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">At each visit</td></tr></tbody></table></div><div><div><dl class="temp-labeled-list small"><dt></dt><dd><div><p class="no_margin">ACTH = adrenocorticotropic hormone; BUN = blood urea nitrogen; OGTT = oral glucose tolerance test; OT = occupational therapy; PT = physical therapy</p></div></dd></dl></div></div></div></div><div id="leigh-nucl-ov.AgentsCircumstances_to_Avo"><h3>Agents/Circumstances to Avoid</h3><p>A Delphi review has examined drug safety in mitochondrial disorders [<a class="bk_pop" href="#leigh-nucl-ov.REF.de_vries.2020.800">De Vries et al 2020</a>]. However, it is important to tailor medication recommendations to each individual.</p><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Sodium valproate should be avoided if possible, because of its inhibitory effect on respiratory chain enzymes. Absolute contraindication in all individuals with <i>POLG</i>-related disease [<a class="bk_pop" href="#leigh-nucl-ov.REF.de_vries.2020.800">De Vries et al 2020</a>].</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Anesthesia can potentially aggravate respiratory manifestations and precipitate respiratory failure; thus, careful consideration should be given to its use and to monitoring the individual prior to, during, and after its use [<a class="bk_pop" href="#leigh-nucl-ov.REF.shear.2004.792">Shear &#x00026; Tobias 2004</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#leigh-nucl-ov.REF.niezgoda.2013.785">Niezgoda &#x00026; Morgan 2013</a>].</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Prolonged propofol use during maintenance anesthesia may increase the risk of lactic acidosis.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Catabolism should be prevented by minimizing preoperative fasting and considering intravenous glucose perioperatively during prolonged anesthesia (unless the individual is on a ketogenic diet).</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Neuromuscular blocking drugs should be avoided in individuals with muscle disease, or if necessary, used under strict monitoring.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Avoid lactate-containing agents (e.g., Ringer's lactate) in those at risk of lactic acidosis [<a class="bk_pop" href="#leigh-nucl-ov.REF.parikh.2017">Parikh et al 2017</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#leigh-nucl-ov.REF.de_vries.2020.800">De Vries et al 2020</a>].</div></li></ul></div></div><div id="leigh-nucl-ov.Genetic_Counseling"><h2 id="_leigh-nucl-ov_Genetic_Counseling_">6.  Genetic Counseling</h2><p><i>Genetic counseling is the process of providing individuals and families with information on the nature, mode(s) of inheritance, and implications of genetic disorders to help them make informed medical and personal decisions. The following section deals with genetic risk assessment and the use of family history and genetic testing to clarify genetic status for family members; it is not meant to address all personal, cultural, or ethical issues that may arise or to substitute for consultation with a genetics professional</i>. &#x02014;ED.</p><div id="leigh-nucl-ov.Mode_of_Inheritance"><h3>Mode of Inheritance</h3><p>Nuclear <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/gene/">gene</a>-encoded Leigh syndrome spectrum (LSS) can be inherited in an <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/autosomal-recessive/">autosomal recessive</a>, <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/x-linked/">X-linked</a>, or <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/autosomal-dominant/">autosomal dominant</a> manner.</p><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Of the more than 115 nuclear <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/gene/">gene</a>-encoded LSS-related genes identified to date, pathogenic variants in all but six genes are associated with <b><a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/autosomal-recessive/">autosomal recessive</a></b> inheritance.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>LSS caused by a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/heterozygous/">heterozygous</a> or <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/hemizygous/">hemizygous</a> <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> in <i>AIFM1</i>, <i>NDUFA1</i>, <i>HSD17B10</i>, or <i>PDHA1</i> is inherited in an <b><a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/x-linked/">X-linked</a></b> manner. Note: Almost equal numbers of males and females affected with <i>PDHA1</i>-related LSS have been reported [<a class="bk_pop" href="#leigh-nucl-ov.REF.lissens.2000.209">Lissens et al 2000</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#leigh-nucl-ov.REF.imbard.2011.507">Imbard et al 2011</a>]. Although relatively few affected individuals with <i>NDUFA1</i>-, <i>HSD17B10</i>-, and <i>AIFM1-</i>related LSS have been reported, it is expected that the same sex ratio would be seen in all four X-linked disorders.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div><i>SLC25A4-</i> and <i>SSBP1-</i>related LSS are <b><a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/autosomal-dominant/">autosomal dominant</a></b> disorders. To date, <i>SSBP1-</i> and <i>SLC25A4</i>-related LSS have each been reported in a single individual and in both instances have occurred as the result of a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/de-novo/"><i>de novo</i></a> <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a>.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Two genes, <i>DNM1L</i> and <i>OPA1</i>, have been associated with both <b><a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/autosomal-dominant/">autosomal dominant</a></b> and <b><a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/autosomal-recessive/">autosomal recessive</a></b> LSS.</div></li></ul><p>Genetic counseling regarding risk to family members depends on accurate diagnosis, determination of the <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/mode-of-inheritance/">mode of inheritance</a> in each family, and results of <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/molecular-genetic-testing/">molecular genetic testing</a>.</p></div><div id="leigh-nucl-ov.Autosomal_Recessive_Inheri"><h3>Autosomal Recessive Inheritance &#x02013; Risk to Family Members</h3><p>
<b>Parents of a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a></b>
</p><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>The parents of an affected child are presumed to be <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/heterozygous/">heterozygous</a> for an LSS-related <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a>.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>If a molecular diagnosis has been established in the <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a>, <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/molecular-genetic-testing/">molecular genetic testing</a> is recommended for the parents of the proband to confirm that both parents are <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/heterozygous/">heterozygous</a> for an LSS-related <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> and to allow reliable <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/recurrence-risk/">recurrence risk</a> assessment.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>If a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> is detected in only one parent and parental identity testing has confirmed biological maternity and paternity, it is possible that one of the pathogenic variants identified in the <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a> occurred as a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/de-novo/"><i>de novo</i></a> event in the proband or as a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/postzygotic/">postzygotic</a> <i>de novo</i> event in a mosaic parent [<a class="bk_pop" href="#leigh-nucl-ov.REF.j_nsson.2017.519">J&#x000f3;nsson et al 2017</a>]. If the proband appears to have <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/homozygous/">homozygous</a> pathogenic variants (i.e., the same two pathogenic variants), additional possibilities to consider include:</div><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>A single- or multiexon <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/deletion/">deletion</a> in the <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a> that was not detected by <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/sequence-analysis/">sequence analysis</a> and that resulted in the artifactual appearance of homozygosity;</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Uniparental isodisomy for the parental <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/chromosome/">chromosome</a> with the <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> that resulted in homozygosity for the pathogenic variant in the <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a>.</div></li></ul></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Heterozygotes (carriers) are asymptomatic and are not at risk of developing the disorder.</div></li></ul><p>
<b>Sibs of a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a></b>
</p><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>If both parents are known to be <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/heterozygous/">heterozygous</a> for an LSS-related <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a>, each sib of an affected individual has at conception a 25% chance of being affected, a 50% chance of being an asymptomatic <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/carrier/">carrier</a>, and a 25% chance of being unaffected and not a carrier.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Heterozygotes (carriers) are asymptomatic and are not at risk of developing the disorder.</div></li></ul><p><b>Offspring of a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a>.</b> Individuals with <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/autosomal-recessive/">autosomal recessive</a> LSS are not known to reproduce.</p><p><b>Other family members.</b> Each sib of the <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a>'s parents is at a 50% risk of being a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/carrier/">carrier</a> of an LSS-related <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a>.</p><p><b>Carrier detection.</b> Carrier testing for at-risk relatives requires prior identification of the LSS-related pathogenic variants in the family.</p></div><div id="leigh-nucl-ov.XLinked_Inheritance__Risk"><h3>X-Linked Inheritance &#x02013; Risk to Family Members</h3><p>
<b>Parents of a male <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a></b>
</p><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>The father of an affected male will not have <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/x-linked/">X-linked</a> LSS nor will he be <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/hemizygous/">hemizygous</a> for an <i>AIFM1</i>, <i>HSD17B10</i>, <i>NDUFA1</i>, or <i>PDHA1</i> <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a>; therefore, he does not require further evaluation/testing.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>In a family with more than one affected individual, the mother of an affected male is an <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/obligate-heterozygote/">obligate heterozygote</a>. Note: If a woman has more than one affected child and no other affected relatives and if the <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/familial/">familial</a> <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> cannot be detected in her leukocyte DNA, she most likely has <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/gonadal-mosaicism/">gonadal mosaicism</a>.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>If a male is the only affected family member (i.e., a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/simplex/">simplex</a> case):</div><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>The mother may be <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/heterozygous/">heterozygous</a> for the <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/x-linked/">X-linked</a> LSS-related <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> or have somatic/<a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/gonadal-mosaicism/">gonadal mosaicism</a>; or</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>The affected male may have a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/de-novo/"><i>de novo</i></a> <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> (in which case the mother is not a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/heterozygote/">heterozygote</a>). (About 60%-63% of males with <i>PDHA1</i>-related primary pyruvate dehydrogenase complex deficiency [PDCD] have the disorder as the result of a <i>de novo</i> pathogenic variant [see <a href="/sites/books/n/gene/pdhc-def-ov/">Primary Pyruvate Dehydrogenase Complex Deficiency Overview</a>]).</div></li></ul></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Molecular genetic testing of the mother is recommended to evaluate her genetic status and inform <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/recurrence-risk/">recurrence risk</a> assessment. Note: Testing of maternal leukocyte DNA may not detect all instances of <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/somatic-mosaicism/">somatic mosaicism</a> and will not detect a <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> that is present in the germ (gonadal) cells only.</div></li></ul><p>
<b>Parents of a female <a class="def" href="/sites/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a></b>
